``expectations``\n              - Ground truth or any expectation for each prediction, e.g. expected retrieved docs.\n              - Derived from either dataset or trace.\n\n                * When the dataset contains ``expectations`` column, the value will be\n                  passed as is.\n                * When traces are provided as evaluation dataset, this will be a dictionary\n                  that contains a set of assessments in the format of\n                  [assessment name]: [assessment value].\n\n            * - ``trace``\n              - A trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row.\n              - Specified as a ``trace`` column in the dataset, or generated during the prediction.\n\n        Example:\n\n            .. code-block:: python\n\n                class NotEmpty(BaseScorer):\n                    name = \"not_empty\"\n\n                    def __call__(self, *, outputs) -> bool:\n                        return outputs != \"\"\n\n\n                class ExactMatch(BaseScorer):\n                    name = \"exact_match\"\n\n                    def __call__(self, *, outputs, expectations) -> bool:\n                        return outputs == expectations[\"expected_response\"]\n\n\n                class NumToolCalls(BaseScorer):\n                    name = \"num_tool_calls\"\n\n                    def __call__(self, *, trace) -> int:\n                        spans = trace.search_spans(name=\"tool_call\")\n                        return len(spans)\n\n\n                # Use the scorer in an evaluation\n                mlflow.genai.evaluate(\n                    data=data,\n                    scorers=[NotEmpty(), ExactMatch(), NumToolCalls()],\n                )\n        \"\"\"\n        raise NotImplementedError(\"Implementation of __call__ is required for Scorer class\")\n\n    @property\n    def kind(self) -> ScorerKind:\n        return ScorerKind.CLASS\n\n    def register(self, *, name: str | None = None, experiment_id: str | None = None) -> \"Scorer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Register this scorer with the MLflow server.\n\n        This method registers the scorer for use with automatic trace evaluation in the\n        specified experiment. Once registered, the scorer can be started to begin\n        evaluating traces automatically.\n\n        Args:\n            name: Optional registered name for the scorer. If not provided, the current `name`\n                property value will be used as a registered name.\n            experiment_id: The ID of the MLflow experiment to register the scorer for.\n                If None, uses the currently active experiment.\n\n        Returns:\n            A new Scorer instance with server registration information.\n\n        Example:\n\n            .. code-block:: python\n\n                import mlflow\n                from mlflow.genai.scorers import RelevanceToQuery\n\n                # Register a built-in scorer\n                mlflow.set_experiment(\"my_genai_app\")\n                registered_scorer = RelevanceToQuery().register(name=\"relevance_scorer\")\n                print(f\"Registered scorer: {registered_scorer.name}\")\n\n                # Register a custom scorer\n                from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\n\n\n                @scorer\n                def custom_length_check(outputs) -> bool:\n                    return len(outputs) > 100\n\n\n                registered_custom = custom_length_check.register(\n                    name=\"output_length_checker\", experiment_id=\"12345\"\n                )\n        \"\"\"\n        # Get the current tracking store\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers.registry import DatabricksStore, _get_scorer_store\n\n        self._check_can_be_registered()\n        store = _get_scorer_store()\n        # Create a new scorer instance\n        new_scorer = self._create_copy()\n\n        # If name is provided, update the copy's name\n        if name:\n            new_scorer.name = name\n            # Update cached dump to reflect the new name\n            if new_scorer._cached_dump is not None:\n                new_scorer._cached_dump[\"name\"] = name\n\n        store.register_scorer(experiment_id, new_scorer)\n\n        if isinstance(store, DatabricksStore):\n            new_scorer._registered_backend = \"databricks\"\n        else:\n            new_scorer._registered_backend = \"tracking\"\n        return new_scorer\n\n    def start(\n        self,\n        *,\n        name: str | None = None,\n        experiment_id: str | None = None,\n        sampling_config: ScorerSamplingConfig,\n    ) -> \"Scorer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Start registered scoring with the specified sampling configuration.\n\n        This method activates automatic trace evaluation for the scorer. The scorer will\n        evaluate traces based on the provided sampling configuration, including the\n        sample rate and optional filter criteria.\n\n        Args:\n            name: Optional scorer name. If not provided, uses the scorer's registered\n                name or default name.\n            experiment_id: The ID of the MLflow experiment containing the scorer.\n                If None, uses the currently active experiment.\n            sampling_config: Configuration object containing:\n                - sample_rate: Fraction of traces to evaluate (0.0 to 1.0). Required.\n                - filter_string: Optional MLflow search_traces compatible filter string.\n\n        Returns:\n            A new Scorer instance with updated sampling configuration.\n\n        Example:\n            .. code-block:: python\n\n                import mlflow\n                from mlflow.genai.scorers import RelevanceToQuery, ScorerSamplingConfig\n\n                # Start scorer with 50% sampling rate\n                mlflow.set_experiment(\"my_genai_app\")\n                scorer = RelevanceToQuery().register()\n                active_scorer = scorer.start(sampling_config=ScorerSamplingConfig(sample_rate=0.5))\n                print(f\"Scorer is evaluating {active_scorer.sample_rate * 100}% of traces\")\n\n                # Start scorer with filter to only evaluate specific traces\n                filtered_scorer = scorer.start(\n                    sampling_config=ScorerSamplingConfig(\n                        sample_rate=1.0, filter_string=\"YOUR_FILTER_STRING\"\n                    )\n                )\n        \"\"\"\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers.registry import DatabricksStore\n        from mlflow.tracking._tracking_service.utils import get_tracking_uri\n        from mlflow.utils.uri import is_databricks_uri\n\n        if not is_databricks_uri(get_tracking_uri()):\n            raise MlflowException(\n                \"Scheduling scorers is only supported by Databricks tracking URI.\"\n            )\n\n        self._check_can_be_registered()\n\n        if sampling_config.sample_rate is not None and sampling_config.sample_rate <= 0:\n            raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n                \"When starting a scorer, provided sample rate must be greater than 0\"\n            )\n\n        scorer_name = name or self.name\n\n        # Update the scorer on the server\n        return DatabricksStore.update_registered_scorer(\n            name=scorer_name,\n            scorer=self,\n            sample_rate=sampling_config.sample_rate,\n            filter_string=sampling_config.filter_string,\n            experiment_id=experiment_id,\n        )\n\n    def update(\n        self,\n        *,\n        name: str | None = None,\n        experiment_id: str | None = None,\n        sampling_config: ScorerSamplingConfig,\n    ) -> \"Scorer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Update the sampling configuration for this scorer.\n\n        This method modifies the sampling rate and/or filter criteria for an already\n        registered scorer. It can be used to dynamically adjust how many traces are\n        evaluated or change the filtering criteria without stopping and restarting\n        the scorer.\n\n        Args:\n            name: Optional scorer name. If not provided, uses the scorer's registered name\n                or default name.\n            experiment_id: The ID of the MLflow experiment containing the scorer.\n                If None, uses the currently active experiment.\n            sampling_config: Configuration object containing:\n                - sample_rate: New fraction of traces to evaluate (0.0 to 1.0). Optional.\n                - filter_string: New MLflow search_traces compatible filter string. Optional.\n\n        Returns:\n            A new Scorer instance with updated configuration.\n\n        Example:\n\n            .. code-block:: python\n\n                import mlflow\n                from mlflow.genai.scorers import RelevanceToQuery, ScorerSamplingConfig\n\n                # Start scorer with initial configuration\n                mlflow.set_experiment(\"my_genai_app\")\n                scorer = RelevanceToQuery().register()\n                active_scorer = scorer.start(sampling_config=ScorerSamplingConfig(sample_rate=0.1))\n\n                # Update to increase sampling rate during high traffic\n                updated_scorer = active_scorer.update(\n                    sampling_config=ScorerSamplingConfig(sample_rate=0.5)\n                )\n                print(f\"Updated sample rate: {updated_scorer.sample_rate}\")\n\n                # Update to add filtering criteria\n                filtered_scorer = updated_scorer.update(\n                    sampling_config=ScorerSamplingConfig(filter_string=\"YOUR_FILTER_STRING\")\n                )\n                print(f\"Added filter: {filtered_scorer.filter_string}\")\n        \"\"\"\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers.registry import DatabricksStore\n        from mlflow.tracking._tracking_service.utils import get_tracking_uri\n        from mlflow.utils.uri import is_databricks_uri\n\n        if not is_databricks_uri(get_tracking_uri()):\n            raise MlflowException(\n                \"Updating scheduled scorers is only supported by Databricks tracking URI.\"\n            )\n\n        self._check_can_be_registered()\n\n        scorer_name = name or self.name\n\n        # Update the scorer on the server\n        return DatabricksStore.update_registered_scorer(\n            name=scorer_name,\n            scorer=self,\n            sample_rate=sampling_config.sample_rate,\n            filter_string=sampling_config.filter_string,\n            experiment_id=experiment_id,\n        )\n\n    def stop(self, *, name: str | None = None, experiment_id: str | None = None) -> \"Scorer\":\n        \"\"\"\n        Stop registered scoring by setting sample rate to 0.\n\n        This method deactivates automatic trace evaluation for the scorer while keeping\n        the scorer registered. If not provided, uses the scorer's registered name\n                or default name.\n            experiment_id: The ID of the MLflow experiment containing the scorer.\n                If None, uses the currently active experiment.\n\n        Returns:\n            A new Scorer instance with sample rate set to 0.\n\n        Example:\n\n            .. code-block:: python\n\n                import mlflow\n                from mlflow.genai.scorers import RelevanceToQuery, ScorerSamplingConfig\n\n                # Start and then stop a scorer\nimport logging\nimport math\nfrom abc import CONTEXT_SUFFICIENCY_PROMPT_INSTRUCTIONS,\n)\nfrom mlflow.genai.judges.prompts.correctness import CORRECTNESS_PROMPT_INSTRUCTIONS\nfrom mlflow.genai.judges.prompts.groundedness import None = None\n    outputs: Any | None = None\n    expectations: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n\n\ndef _construct_field_extraction_config(\n    needs_inputs: bool,\n    needs_outputs: bool,\n) -> FieldExtractionConfig:\n    \"\"\"\n    Construct field extraction configuration with messages and schema.\n\n    Args:\n        needs_inputs: Whether inputs field needs extraction.\n        needs_outputs: Whether outputs field needs extraction.\n\n    Returns:\n        FieldExtractionConfig containing messages and schema for extraction.\n    \"\"\"\n    from mlflow.types.llm import ChatMessage\n\n    extraction_tasks = []\n    schema_fields = {}\n\n    if needs_inputs:\n        extraction_tasks.append('- \"inputs\": The initial user request/question')\n        schema_fields[\"inputs\"] = (\n            str,\n            pydantic.Field(\n                description='The user\\'s original request (field name must be exactly \"inputs\")'\n            ),\n          description='The system\\'s final response (field name must be exactly \"outputs\")'\n            ),\n        )\n\n    schema = pydantic.create_model(\"ExtractionSchema\", **schema_fields)\n\n    # Build example field names for the IMPORTANT message\n    example_fields = []\n    if needs_inputs:\n        example_fields.append('\"inputs\"')\n    if needs_outputs:\n        example_fields.append('\"outputs\"')\n    example_text = \", \".join(example_fields)\n\n    messages = [\n        ChatMessage(\n            role=\"system\",\n            content=(\n                \"Extract the following fields from the trace.\\n\"\n                \"Use the provided tools to examine the trace's spans to find:\\n\"\n                + \"\\n\".join(extraction_tasks)\n Do not use singular forms or \"\n                + \"variations of these field names.\"\n            ),\n        ),\n        ChatMessage(\n            role=\"user\",\n            content=(\n                \"Use the tools to find the required fields, then return them as JSON \"\n                \"with the exact field names specified.\"\n            ),\n        ),\n    ]\n\n    return FieldExtractionConfig(messages=messages, schema=schema)\n\n\ndef _validate_required_fields(\n    fields: ExtractedFields,\n    judge: Judge,\n    scorer_name: str,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate that all required fields for a scorer are present.\n\n    Args:\n        fields: Extracted fields containing inputs, outputs, and expectations.\n        judge: Judge instance to determine which fields are required.\n        scorer_name: Name of the scorer for error messages.\n\n    Raises:\n        MlflowException: If any required fields are missing.\n    \"\"\"\n    required_fields = {field.name for field in judge.get_input_fields()}\n    missing_fields = []\n\n    if \"inputs\" in required_fields and fields.inputs is None:\n        missing_fields.append(\"inputs\")\n    if \"outputs\" in required_fields and fields.outputs is None:\n        missing_fields.append(\"outputs\")\n    if \"expectations\" in required_fields and fields.expectations is None:\n        missing_fields.append(\"expectations\")\n\n    if missing_fields:\n        fields_str = \", \".join(missing_fields)\n        raise MlflowException(\n            f\"{scorer_name} requires the following fields: {fields_str}. \"\n            \"Provide them directly or pass a trace containing them.\"\n        )\n\n\ndef resolve_scorer_fields(\n    trace: Trace | None,\n    judge: Judge,\n    inputs: Any | None = None,\n    outputs: Any | None = None,\n    expectations: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    model: str | None = None,\n    extract_expectations: bool = False,\n) -> ExtractedFields:\n    \"\"\"\n    Resolve scorer fields from provided values or extract from trace if needed.\n\n    Args:\n        trace: MLflow trace object containing the execution to evaluate.\n        judge: Judge instance to determine which fields need extraction.\n        inputs: Input data to evaluate. If None, will be extracted from trace.\n        outputs: Output data to evaluate. If None, will be extracted from trace.\n        expectations: Dictionary of expected outcomes. If None, will be extracted from trace.\n        model: Model URI to use for LLM-based extraction if needed.\n        extract_expectations: If True, extract expectations from trace.\n\n    Returns:\n        ExtractedFields dataclass containing inputs, outputs, and expectations\n    \"\"\"\n    if not trace:\n        return ExtractedFields(inputs=inputs, outputs=outputs, expectations=expectations)\n\n    inputs = resolve_inputs_from_trace(inputs, trace)\n    outputs = resolve_outputs_from_trace(outputs, trace)\n    if extract_expectations:\n        expectations = resolve_expectations_from_trace(expectations, trace)\n\n    input_field_names = {field.name for field in judge.get_input_fields()}\n    needs_inputs = inputs is None and \"inputs\" in input_field_names\n    needs_outputs = outputs is None and \"outputs\" in input_field_names\n\n    if needs_inputs or needs_outputs:\n        extraction_config = _construct_field_extraction_config(\n            needs_inputs=needs_inputs,\n            needs_outputs=needs_outputs,\n        )\n\n        try:\n            extracted = get_chat_completions_with_structured_output(\n                model_uri=model or get_default_model(),\n                messages=extraction_config.messages,\n                output_schema=extraction_config.schema,\n                trace=trace,\n            )\n            if needs_inputs:\n                inputs = inputs or extracted.inputs\n            if needs_outputs:\n                outputs = outputs or extracted.outputs\n        except Exception as e:\n            _logger.warning(\n                \"Failed to extract required fields from trace using LLM: %s\",\n                e,\n            )\n\n    return ExtractedFields(inputs=inputs, outputs=outputs, expectations=expectations)\n\n\ndef _sanitize_scorer_feedback(feedback: Feedback) -> Feedback:\n    \"\"\"Sanitize feedback values from LLM judges to ensure YES/NO consistency.\"\"\"\n    if feedback.value:\n        if isinstance(feedback.value, CategoricalRating):\n            return feedback\n\n        if isinstance(feedback.value, str):\n            value_str = feedback.value.strip().lower()\n\n            if value_str in _AFFIRMATIVE_VALUES:\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating.YES\n            elif value_str in _NEGATIVE_VALUES:\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating.NO\n            else:\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating(value_str)\n\n    return feedback\n\n\nclass BuiltInScorer(Judge):\n    \"\"\"\n set()\n\n    @property\n    @abstractmethod\n    def instructions(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the instructions of what this scorer evaluates.\n        \"\"\"\n\n    def model_dump(self, **kwargs) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Override model_dump to handle builtin scorer serialization.\"\"\"\n        pydantic_model_data = pydantic.BaseModel.model_dump(self, mode=\"json\", **kwargs)\n        pydantic_model_data[\"instructions\"] = self.instructions\n\n        serialized =           [assessment name]: [assessment value].\n\n        * - ``trace``\n          - A trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row.\n          - Specified as a ``trace`` column in the dataset, or generated during the prediction.\n\n    The scorer function should return one of the following:\n\n    * A boolean value\n    * An integer value\n    * A float value\n    * A string value\n    * A single :class:`~mlflow.entities.Feedback` object\n    * A list of :class:`~mlflow.entities.Feedback` objects\n\n    .. note::\n\n        The metric name will be determined by the scorer function's name or a custom name\n        specified in the `name` parameter for the scorer.\n\n    Args:\n        func: The scorer function to be decorated.\n        name: The name of the scorer.\n        description: A description of what the scorer evaluates.\n        aggregations: A list of aggregation functions to apply to the scorer's output.\n            The aggregation functions can be either a string or a callable.\n\n            * If a string, it must be one of `[\"min\", \"max\", \"mean\", \"median\", \"variance\", \"p90\"]`.\n            * If a callable, it must take a list of values and return a single value.\n\n            By default, \"mean\" is used as the aggregation function.\n\n    Example:\n\n        .. code-block:: python\n\n            import json\n            from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\n            from mlflow.entities import AssessmentSource, Feedback\n\n\n            # Basic scorers that returns primitive values\n            @scorer\n            def not_empty(outputs) -> bool:\n                return outputs != \"\"\n\n\n            @scorer\n            def exact_match(outputs, expectations) -> bool:\n                return outputs == expectations[\"expected_response\"]\n\n\n            @scorer\n            def num_tool_calls(trace) -> int:\n                spans = trace.search_spans(name=\"tool_call\")\n                return len(spans)\n\n\n            # Use `Feedback` object to return additional information about the scorer's\n            # result, such as a rationale for the score.\n            @scorer\n            def harmfulness(outputs) -> Feedback:\n                import openai\n\n                prompt = f'''\n                    Judge if the following text is harmful or not.\n\n                    Text:\n                    {outputs}\n\n                    Return the answer in a JSON object with the following format:\n                    {{\n                        \"harmful\": true\n                        \"reason\": \"The text contains harmful content\"\n                    }}\n\n                    Do not output any other characters than the json object.\n                '''\n                response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\n                    model=\"o4-mini\",\n                    messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": prompt}],\n                )\n                payload = json.loads(response.choices[0].message.content)\n                return Feedback(\n                    value=payload[\"harmful\"],\n                    rationale=payload[\"reason\"],\n                    source=AssessmentSource(\n                        source_type=\"LLM_JUDGE\",\n                        source_id=\"openai:/o4-mini\",\n                    ),\n                )\n\n\n            # Use the scorer in an evaluation\n            mlflow.genai.evaluate(\n                data=data,\n                scorers=[not_empty, exact_match, num_tool_calls, harmfulness],\n            )\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if func is None:\n        return functools.partial(\n            scorer, name=name, description=description, aggregations=aggregations\n        )\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n    # Update the __call__ method's signature to match the original function\n    # but add 'self' as the first parameter. This is required for MLflow to\n    # pass the correct set of parameters to the scorer.\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating.YES\n            elif value_str in _NEGATIVE_VALUES:\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating.NO\n            else:\n                feedback.value = CategoricalRating(value_str)\n\n    return feedback\n\n\nclass BuiltInScorer(Judge):\n    \"\"\"\n    Abstract base class for built-in scorers that share a common implementation.\n    All built-in scorers should inherit from this class.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    required_columns: set[str] = set()\n\n    @property\n    @abstractmethod\n    def instructions(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the instructions of what this scorer evaluates.\n        \"\"\"\n\n    def model_dump(self, **kwargs) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Override model_dump to handle builtin scorer serialization.\"\"\"\n        pydantic_model_data = pydantic.BaseModel.model_dump(self, mode=\"json\", **kwargs)\n        pydantic_model_data[\"instructions\"] = self.instructions\n\n        serialized = SerializedScorer(\n            name=self.name,\n            description=self.description,\n            aggregations=self.aggregations,\n            mlflow_version=mlflow.__version__,\n            serialization_version=_SERIALIZATION_VERSION,\n            builtin_scorer_class=self.__class__.__name__,\n            builtin_scorer_pydantic_data=pydantic_model_data,\n        )\n\n        return asdict(serialized)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def model_validate(cls, obj: SerializedScorer | dict[str, Any]) -> \"BuiltInScorer\":\n        \"\"\"Override model_validate to handle builtin scorer deserialization.\"\"\"\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import builtin_scorers\n\n        if isinstance(obj, SerializedScorer):\n            serialized = obj\n        else:\n            if not isinstance(obj, dict) or \"builtin_scorer_class\" not in obj:\n                raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n                    f\"Invalid builtin scorer data: expected a dictionary with \"\n                    f\"'builtin_scorer_class' field, got {type(obj).__name__}.\"\n                )\n\n            try:\n                serialized = SerializedScorer(**obj)\n            except Exception as e:\n                raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n                    f\"Failed to parse serialized scorer data: {e}\"\n                )\n\n        try:\n            scorer_class = getattr(builtin_scorers, serialized.builtin_scorer_class)\n        except AttributeError:\n            raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n                f\"Unknown builtin scorer class: {serialized.builtin_scorer_class}\"\n            )\n\n        constructor_args = serialized.builtin_scorer_pydantic_data or {}\n\n        return scorer_class(**constructor_args)\n\n    def validate_columns(self, columns: set[str]) -> None:\n        missing_columns = self.required_columns - columns\n        if missing_columns:\n            raise MissingColumnsException(self.name, missing_columns)\n\n    @property\n    def kind(self) -> ScorerKind:\n        return ScorerKind.BUILTIN\n\n\n@format_docstring(_MODEL_API_DOC)\nclass RetrievalRelevance(BuiltInScorer):\n    \"\"\"\n    Retrieval relevance measures whether each chunk is relevant to the input request.\n\n    You can invoke the scorer directly with a single input for testing, or pass it to\n    `mlflow.genai.evaluate` for running full evaluation on a dataset.\n\n    Args:\n        name: The name of the scorer. Defaults to \"retrieval_relevance\".\n        model: {{ model }}\n\n    Example (direct usage):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import RetrievalRelevance\n\n        trace = mlflow.get_trace(\"<your-trace-id>\")\n        feedbacks = RetrievalRelevance(name=\"my_retrieval_relevance\")(trace=trace)\n        print(feedbacks)\n\n    Example (with evaluate):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n\n        data = mlflow.search_traces(...)\n        result = mlflow.genai.evaluate(data=data, scorers=[RetrievalRelevance()])\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str = \"retrieval_relevance\"\n    model: str | None = None\n    required_columns: set[str] = {\"inputs\", \"trace\"}\n    description: str = (\n        \"Evaluate whether each retrieved context chunk is relevant to the input request.\"\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self, /, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n    @property\n    def instructions(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Get the instructions of what this scorer evaluates.\"\"\"\n        return \"Evaluates whether each retrieved context chunk is relevant to the input request.\"\n\n    def get_input_fields(self) -> list[JudgeField]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the input fields for the RetrievalRelevance judge.\n\n        Returns:\n            List of JudgeField objects defining the input fields based on the __call__ method.\n        \"\"\"\n        return [\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"trace\",\n                description=(\n                    \"The trace of the model's execution. Must contains at least one span with \"\n                    \"type `RETRIEVER`. MLflow will extract the retrieved context from that span. \"\n                    \"If multiple spans are found, MLflow will use the **last** one.\"\n                ),\n            ),\n        ]\n\n    def __call__(self, *, trace: Trace) -> Feedback:\n        \"\"\"\n        Evaluate chunk relevance for each context chunk.\n\n        Args:\n            trace: The trace of the model's execution. Must contains at least one span with\n                type `RETRIEVER`. MLflow will extract the retrieved context from that span.\n                If multiple spans are found, MLflow will use the **last** one.\n\n        Returns:\n            A list of assessments evaluating the relevance of each context chunk.\n            If the number of retrievers is N and each retriever has M chunks, the list will\n            contain N * (M + 1) assessments. Each retriever span will emit M assessments\n            for the relevance of its chunks and 1 assessment for the average relevance of all\n            chunks.\n        \"\"\"\n        request = extract_request_from_trace(trace)\n        span_id_to_context = extract_retrieval_context_from_trace(trace)\n\n        feedbacks = []\n        for span_id, context in span_id_to_context.items():\n            feedbacks.extend(self._compute_span_relevance(span_id, request, context))\n        return feedbacks\n\n    def _compute_span_relevance(\n        self, span_id: str, request: str, chunks: list[dict[str, str]]\n    ) -> list[Feedback]:\n        \"\"\"Compute the relevance of retrieved context for one retriever span.\"\"\"\n        from mlflow.genai.judges.prompts.retrieval_relevance import get_prompt\n\n        model = self.model or get_default_model()\n\n        chunk_feedbacks = []\n        if model == \"databricks\":\n            from databricks.agents.evals.judges import chunk_relevance\n\n            chunk_feedbacks = chunk_relevance(\n                request=request, retrieved_context=chunks, assessment_name=self.name\n            )\n        else:\n            for chunk in chunks:\n                prompt = get_prompt(request=request, context=chunk[\"content\"])\n                feedback = invoke_judge_model(model, prompt, assessment_name=self.name)\n                sanitized_feedback = _sanitize_scorer_feedback(feedback)\n                chunk_feedbacks.append(sanitized_feedback)\n\n        for feedback in chunk_feedbacks:\n            feedback.span_id = span_id\n\n        if len(chunk_feedbacks) == 0:\n            return []\n\n        average = sum(f.value == \"yes\" for f in chunk_feedbacks) / len(chunk_feedbacks)\n\n        span_level_feedback = Feedback(\n            name=self.name + \"/precision\",\n            value=average,\n            source=chunk_feedbacks[0].source,\n            span_id=span_id,\n        )\n        return [span_level_feedback] + chunk_feedbacks\n\n\n@format_docstring(_MODEL_API_DOC)\nclass RetrievalSufficiency(BuiltInScorer):\n    \"\"\"\n    Retrieval sufficiency evaluates whether the retrieved documents provide all necessary\n    information to generate the expected response.\n\n    You can invoke the scorer directly with a single input for testing, or pass it to\n    `mlflow.genai.evaluate` for running full evaluation on a dataset.\n\n    Args:\n        name: The name of the scorer. Defaults to \"retrieval_sufficiency\".\n        model: {{ model }}\n\n    Example (direct usage):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import RetrievalSufficiency\n\n        trace = mlflow.get_trace(\"<your-trace-id>\")\n        feedback = RetrievalSufficiency(name=\"my_retrieval_sufficiency\")(trace=trace)\n        print(feedback)\n\n    Example (with evaluate):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n\n        data = mlflow.search_traces(...)\n        result = mlflow.genai.evaluate(data=data, scorers=[RetrievalSufficiency()])\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str = \"retrieval_sufficiency\"\n    model: str | None = None\n    required_columns: set[str] = {\"inputs\", \"trace\"}\n    description: str = (\n        \"Evaluate whether the information in the last retrieval is sufficient to generate \"\n        \"the facts in expected_response or expected_facts.\"\n    )\n\n    @property\n    def instructions(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Get the instructions of what this scorer evaluates.\"\"\"\n        return CONTEXT_SUFFICIENCY_PROMPT_INSTRUCTIONS\n\n    def get_input_fields(self) -> list[JudgeField]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the input fields for the RetrievalSufficiency judge.\n\n        Returns:\n            List of JudgeField objects defining the input fields based on the __call__ method.\n        \"\"\"\n        return [\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"trace\",\n                description=(\n                    \"The trace of the model's execution. Must contain at least one span with \"\n                    \"type `RETRIEVER`. MLflow will extract the retrieved context from that span. \"\n                    \"If multiple spans are found, MLflow will use the **last** one.\"\n                ),\n            ),\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"expectations\",\n                description=(\n                    \"A dictionary of expectations for the response. This must contain either \"\n                    \"`expected_response` or `expected_facts` key (optional).\"\n                ),\n            ),\n        ]\n\n    def validate_columns(self, columns: set[str]) -> None:\n        super().validate_columns(columns)\n        if (\n            \"expectations/expected_response\" not in columns\n            and \"expectations/expected_facts\" not in columns\n        ):\n            raise MissingColumnsException(\n                self.name,\n                [\"expectations/expected_response or expectations/expected_facts\"],\n            )\n\n    def __call__(\n        self, *, trace: Trace, expectations: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n    ) -> list[Feedback]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Evaluate context sufficiency based on retrieved documents.\n\n        Args:\n            trace: The trace of the model's execution. Must contains at least one span with\n                type `RETRIEVER`. MLflow will extract the retrieved context from that span.\n                If multiple spans are found, MLflow will use the **last** one.\n            expectations: A dictionary of expectations for the response. Either `expected_facts` or\n                `expected_response` key is required. Alternatively, you can pass a trace annotated\n                with `expected_facts` or `expected_response` label(s) and omit this argument.\n        \"\"\"\n        request = extract_request_from_trace(trace)\n        span_id_to_context = extract_retrieval_context_from_trace(trace)\n\n        expectations = expectations or {}\n        expected_facts = expectations.get(\"expected_facts\")\n        expected_response = expectations.get(\"expected_response\")\n        if expected_facts is None or expected_response is None:\n            for assessment in trace.info.assessments:\n                if assessment.name == \"expected_facts\" and expected_facts is None:\n                    expected_facts = assessment.value\n                if assessment.name == \"expected_response\" and expected_response is None:\n                    expected_response = assessment.value\n\n        feedbacks = []\n        for span_id, context in span_id_to_context.items():\n            feedback = judges.is_context_sufficient(\n                request=request,\n                context=context,\n                expected_response=expected_response,\n                expected_facts=expected_facts,\n                name=self.name,\n\n        Returns:\n            List of JudgeField objects defining the input fields.\n        \"\"\"\n        return [\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"outputs\",\n                description=\"The actual output from the program to compare.\",\n            ),\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"expectations\",\n                description=(\n                    \"A dictionary containing the expected output. Must contain an \"\n                    \"'expected_response' key with the expected value, e.g. \"\n                    \"{'expected_response': 'Paris'}.\"\n                ),\n            ),\n        ]\n\n    def __call__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        inputs: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        outputs: Any | None = None,\n        expectations: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n        trace: Trace | None = None,\n    ) -> Feedback:\n        \"\"\"\n        Evaluate output equivalence.\n\n        This scorer can be used in two ways:\n        1. Pass an MLflow trace object to automatically extract\n           outputs and expectations from the trace and its assessments.\n        2. Directly provide outputs and expectations to evaluate.\n\n        Args:\n            inputs: A dictionary of input data (optional, not used in evaluation).\n            outputs: The actual output to compare. Optional when trace is provided.\n            expectations: A dictionary containing the expected output. Must contain an\n                'expected_response' key. Optional when trace is provided.\n            trace: MLflow trace object containing the execution to evaluate. When provided,\n                outputs and expectations will be automatically extracted from the trace.\n\n        Returns:\n            Feedback object with 'yes'/'no' value and rationale\n        \"\"\"\n        from mlflow.genai.judges.builtin import _sanitize_feedback\n        from mlflow.genai.judges.prompts.equivalence import (\n            EQUIVALENCE_FEEDBACK_NAME,\n            get_prompt,\n        )\n\n        # Use resolve_scorer_fields to extract fields from trace if provided\n        fields = resolve_scorer_fields(\n            trace,\n            self,\n            inputs,\n            outputs,\n            expectations,\n            model=self.model,\n            extract_expectations=True,\n        )\n        _validate_required_fields(fields, self, \"Equivalence scorer\")\n\n        # Validate that expected_response is present\n        if not fields.expectations or fields.expectations.get(\"expected_response\") is None:\n            raise MlflowException(\n                \"Equivalence scorer requires `expected_response` in the `expectations` dictionary.\"\n            )\n\n        # Extract the expected response\n        expected_output = fields.expectations.get(\"expected_response\")\n        actual_output = fields.outputs\n\n        # Handle exact match for numerical types\n        if isinstance(actual_output, (int, float, bool)) and isinstance(\n            expected_output, (int, float, bool)\n        ):\n            if math.isclose(actual_output, expected_output):\n                return Feedback(\n                    name=self.name,\n                    value=CategoricalRating.YES,\n                    rationale=\"Exact numerical match\",\n                )\n            else:\n                return Feedback(\n                    name=self.name,\n                    value=CategoricalRating.NO,\n                    rationale=f\"Values do not match: {actual_output} != {expected_output}\",\n                )\n\n        # Convert to strings for comparison\n        outputs_str = str(actual_output)\n        expectations_str = str(expected_output)\n\n        # Use exact match first\n        if outputs_str == expectations_str:\n            return Feedback(\n                name=self.name,\n                value=CategoricalRating.YES,\n                rationale=\"Exact string match\",\n            )\n\n        # Use LLM judge for semantic equivalence\n\n        model = self.model or get_default_model()\n        assessment_name = self.name or EQUIVALENCE_FEEDBACK_NAME\n\n        prompt = get_prompt(\n            output=outputs_str,\n            expected_output=expectations_str,\n        )\n        feedback = invoke_judge_model(model, prompt, assessment_name=assessment_name)\n\n        return _sanitize_feedback(feedback)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nclass MissingColumnsException(MlflowException):\n    def __init__(self, scorer: str, missing_columns: set[str]):\n        self.scorer = scorer\n        self.missing_columns = list(missing_columns)\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"The following columns are required for the scorer {scorer}: {missing_columns}\"\n        )from collections import defaultdict\nfrom unittest.mock import call, patch\n\nimport pandas as pd\nimport pytest\n\nimport mlflow\nfrom mlflow.entities import Assessment, AssessmentSource, AssessmentSourceType, Feedback\nfrom mlflow.entities.assessment_error import AssessmentError\nfrom mlflow.genai import Scorer, scorer\nfrom mlflow.genai.judges import make_judge\nfrom mlflow.genai.judges.utils import CategoricalRating\nfrom mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Guidelines, RetrievalGroundedness\nfrom mlflow.genai.scorers.base import SerializedScorer\nfrom mlflow.genai.scorers.registry import get_scorer, list_scorers\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)\ndef increase_db_pool_size(monkeypatch):\n    # Set larger pool size for tests to handle concurrent trace creation\n    # test_extra_traces_from_customer_scorer_should_be_cleaned_up test requires this\n    # to reduce flakiness\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"MLFLOW_SQLALCHEMYSTORE_POOL_SIZE\", \"20\")\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"MLFLOW_SQLALCHEMYSTORE_MAX_OVERFLOW\", \"40\")\n    return\n\n\ndef always_yes(inputs, outputs, expectations, trace):\n    return \"yes\"\n\n\nclass AlwaysYesScorer(Scorer):\n    def __call__(self, inputs, outputs, expectations, trace):\n        return \"yes\"\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef sample_data():\n    return pd.DataFrame(\n        {\n            \"inputs\": [\n                {\"message\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"What is Spark??\"}]},\n                {\n                    \"messages\": [\n                        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"How can you minimize data shuffling in Spark?\"}\n                    ]\n                },\n            ],\n            \"outputs\": [\n                {\"choices\": [{\"message\": {\"content\": \"actual response for first question\"}}]},\n                {\"choices\": [{\"message\": {\"content\": \"actual response for for metric in result.metrics.keys())\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"dummy_scorer\", [AlwaysYesScorer(name=\"always_no\"), scorer(name=\"always_no\")(always_yes)]\n)\ndef test_scorer_name_works(sample_data, dummy_scorer, is_in_databricks):\n    _SCORER_NAME = \"always_no\"\n    result = mlflow.genai.evaluate(data=sample_data, scorers=[dummy_scorer])\n    assert any(_SCORER_NAME in metric for metric in result.metrics.keys())\n\n\ndef test_trace_passed_to_builtin_scorers_correctly(\n    sample_rag_trace, is_in_databricks, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n):\n mock_groundedness.call_count == 2  # Called per retriever span\n\n    mock_correctness.assert_called_once_with(\n        request=\"{'question': 'query'}\",\n        response=\"answer\",\n        expected_facts=[\"fact1\", \"fact2\"],\n        expected_response=\"expected answer\",\n        assessment_name=\"correctness\",\n    )\n    mock_guidelines.assert_called_once_with(\n mock_groundedness.assert_has_calls(\n        [\n            call(\n                request=\"{'question': 'query'}\",\n                response=\"answer\",\n                retrieved_context=[\n                    {\"content\": \"content_1\", \"doc_uri\": \"url_1\"},\n                    {\"content\": \"content_2\", \"doc_uri\": \"url_2\"},\n                ],\n                assessment_name=\"retrieval_groundedness\",\n            ),\n            call(\n                request=\"{'question': 'query'}\",\n                response=\"answer\",\n                retrieved_context=[\n                    {\"content\": \"content_3\"},\n                ],\n                assessment_name=\"retrieval_groundedness\",\n            ),\n        ]\n    )\n\n\ndef test_trace_passed_to_custom_scorer_correctly(sample_data, is_in_databricks):\n    if not is_in_databricks:\n        pytest.skip(\"OSS GenAI evaluator doesn't support passing traces yet\")\n\n    actual_call_args_list = []\n\n    @scorer\n    def dummy_scorer(inputs, outputs, expectations, trace) -> float:\n        actual_call_args_list.append(\n            {\n                \"inputs\": inputs,\n                \"outputs\": outputs,\n                \"expectations\": expectations,\n            }\n        )\n        return 0.0\n\n    mlflow.genai.evaluate(data=sample_data, scorers=[dummy_scorer])\n\n    assert len(actual_call_args_list) == len(sample_data)\n\n    # Prepare expected arguments, keyed by expected_response for matching\n    sample_data_set = defaultdict(set)\n    for i in range(len(sample_data)):\n        sample_data_set[\"inputs\"].add(str(sample_data[\"inputs\"][i]))\n        sample_data_set[\"outputs\"].add(str(sample_data[\"outputs\"][i]))\n        sample_data_set[\"expectations\"].add(\n            str(sample_data[\"expectations\"][i][\"expected_response\"])\n        )\n\n    for actual_args in actual_call_args_list:\n        # do any check since actual passed input could be reformatted and larger than sample input\n        assert any(\n            sample_data_input in str(actual_args[\"inputs\"])\n            for sample_data_input in sample_data_set[\"inputs\"]\n        )\n        assert str(actual_args[\"outputs\"]) in sample_data_set[\"outputs\"]\n        assert (\n            str(actual_args[\"expectations\"][\"expected_response\"]) in sample_data_set[\"expectations\"]\n        )\n\n\ndef test_trace_passed_correctly(is_in_databricks):\n    if not is_in_databricks:\n        pytest.skip(\"OSS GenAI evaluator doesn't support passing traces yet\")\n\n    @mlflow.trace\n    def predict_fn(question):\n        return \"output: \" + str(question)\n\n    actual_call_args_list = []\n\n    @scorer\n    def dummy_scorer(inputs, outputs, trace):\n        actual_call_args_list.append(\n            {\n                \"inputs\": inputs,\n                \"outputs\": outputs,\n                \"trace\": trace,\n            }\n        )\n        return 0.0\n\n    data = [\n        {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"input1\"}},\n        {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"input2\"}},\n    ]\n    mlflow.genai.evaluate(\n        predict_fn=predict_fn,\n        data=data,\n        scorers=[dummy_scorer],\n    )\n\n    assert len(actual_call_args_list) == len(data)\n    for actual_args in actual_call_args_list:\n        assert actual_args[\"trace\"] is not None\n        trace = actual_args[\"trace\"]\n        # check if the input is present in the trace\n        assert any(\n            str(data[i][\"inputs\"][\"question\"]) in str(trace.data.request) for i in range(len(data))\n        )\n        # check if predict_fn was run by making output it starts with \"output:\"\n        assert \"output:\" in str(trace.data.response)[:10]\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"scorer_return\",\n    [\n        \"yes\",\n        42,\n        42.0,\n        # Feedback object.\n        Feedback(name=\"big_question\", value=42, rationale=\"It's the answer to everything\"),\n        # List of Feedback objects.\n        [\n            Feedback(name=\"big_question\", value=42, rationale=\"It's the answer to everything\"),\n            Feedback(name=\"small_question\", value=1, rationale=\"Not sure, just a guess\"),\n        ],\n    ],\n)\ndef test_scorer_on_genai_evaluate(sample_data, scorer_return, is_in_databricks):\n    @scorer\n    def dummy_scorer(inputs, outputs):\n        return scorer_return\n\n    results = mlflow.genai.evaluate(\n        data=sample_data,\n        scorers=[dummy_scorer],\n    )\n    if isinstance(scorer_return, Assessment):\n        assert any(scorer_return.name in metric for metric in results.metrics.keys())\n    elif isinstance(scorer_return, list) and all(\n        isinstance(item, Assessment) for item in scorer_return\n    ):\n        assert any(\n            item.name in metric for item in scorer_return for metric in results.metrics.keys()\n        )\n    else:\n        assert any(\"dummy_scorer\" in metric for metric in results.metrics.keys())\n\n\ndef test_custom_scorer_allow_none_return():\n    @scorer\n    def dummy_scorer(inputs, outputs):\n        return None\n\n    assert dummy_scorer.run(inputs={\"question\": \"query\"}, outputs=\"answer\") is None\n\n\ndef test_scorer_returns_feedback_with_error(sample_data, is_in_databricks):\n    @scorer\n    def dummy_scorer(inputs):\n        return Feedback(\n            name=\"feedback_with_error\",\n            error=AssessmentError(error_code=\"500\", error_message=\"This is an error\"),\n            source=AssessmentSource(source_type=AssessmentSourceType.LLM_JUDGE, source_id=\"gpt\"),\n            metadata={\"index\": 0},\n        )\n\n    results = mlflow.genai.evaluate(\n        data=sample_data,\n        scorers=[dummy_scorer],\n    )\n\n    # Scorer should not be in result when it returns an error\n    assert all(\"dummy_scorer\" not in metric for metric in results.metrics.keys())\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"scorer_return\", \"expected_feedback_name\"),\n    [\n        # Single feedback object with default name -> should be renamed to \"my_scorer\"\n        (Feedback(value=42, rationale=\"rationale\"), \"my_scorer\"),\n        # Single feedback object with custom name -> should NOT be renamed to \"my_scorer\"\n        (Feedback(name=\"custom_name\", value=42, rationale=\"rationale\"), \"custom_name\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_custom_scorer_overwrites_default_feedback_name(scorer_return, expected_feedback_name):\n    @scorer\n    def my_scorer(inputs, outputs):\n        return scorer_return\n\n    feedback = my_scorer.run(\n        inputs={\"question\": \"What is the capital of France?\"},\n        outputs=\"The capital of France is Paris.\",\n    )\n    assert feedback.name == expected_feedback_name\n    assert feedback.value == 42\n\n\ndef test_custom_scorer_does_not_overwrite_feedback_name_when_returning_list():\n    @scorer\n    def my_scorer(inputs, outputs):\n        return [\n            Feedback(name=\"big_question\", value=42, rationale=\"It's the answer to everything\"),\n            Feedback(name=\"small_question\", value=1, rationale=\"Not sure, just a guess\"),\n        ]\n\n    feedbacks = my_scorer.run(\n        inputs={\"question\": \"What is the capital of France?\"},\n        outputs=\"The capital of France is Paris.\",\n    )\n    assert feedbacks[0].name == \"big_question\"\n    assert feedbacks[1].name Please provide a non-empty dataset.\"\n        )\n\n    if not any(col in df.columns for col in (\"trace\", \"inputs\")):\n        raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n            \"Either `inputs` or `trace` column is required in the dataset. Please provide inputs \"\n            \"for every datapoint or provide a trace.\"\n        )\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef _convert_to_eval_set(data: \"EvaluationDatasetTypes\") -> \"pd.DataFrame\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Takes in a dataset in the multiple format that mlflow.genai.evaluate() expects and converts it\n    into standardized Pandas DataFrame.\n    The expected schema can be found at:\n    https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/generative-ai/agent-evaluation/evaluation-schema\n\n    NB: The harness secretly support 'expectations' column as well. It accepts a dictionary of\n        expectations, which is same as the schema that mlflow.genai.evaluate() expects.\n        Therefore, we can simply pass through expectations column.\n    \"\"\"\n    column_mapping = {\n        \"inputs\": \"request\",\n        \"outputs\": \"response\",\n    }\n\n    df = _convert_eval_set_to_df(data)\n\n    return (\n        df.rename(columns=column_mapping)\n        .pipe(_deserialize_trace_column_if_needed)\n        .pipe(_extract_request_response_from_trace)\n        .pipe(_extract_expectations_from_trace)\n    )\n\n\ndef _deserialize_inputs_and_expectations_column(df: \"pd.DataFrame\") -> \"pd.DataFrame\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Deserialize the `inputs` and `expectations` string columns from the dataframe.\n\n    When managed datasets are read as Spark DataFrames, the `inputs` and `expectations` columns\n    are loaded as string columns of JSON strings. This function deserializes these columns into\n    dictionaries expected by mlflow.genai.evaluate().\n    \"\"\"\n    target_columns = [\"inputs\", \"expectations\"]\n    for col in target_columns:\n        if col not in df.columns or not isinstance(df[col][0], str):\n            continue\n\n        try:\n            df[col] = df[col].apply(json.loads)\n        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n            if col == \"inputs\":\n                msg = (\n                    \"The `inputs` column must be a valid JSON string of field names and values. \"\n                    \"For example, `{'question': 'What is the capital of France?'}`\"\n                )\n            else:\n                msg = (\n                    \"The `expectations` column must be a valid JSON string of assessment names and \"\n                    \"values. For example, `{'expected_facts': ['fact1', 'fact2']}`\"\n                )\n            raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n                f\"Failed to parse `{col}` column. Error: {e}\\nHint: {msg}\"\n            )\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef _deserialize_trace_column_if_needed(df: \"pd.DataFrame\") -> \"pd.DataFrame\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Deserialize the `trace` column from the dataframe if it is a string.\n\n    Since MLflow 3.2.0, mlflow.search_traces() returns a pandas DataFrame with a `trace`\n    column that is a trace json representation rather than the Trace object itself. This\n    function deserializes the `trace` column into a Trace object.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"trace\" in df.columns:\n        df[\"trace\"] = df[\"trace\"].apply(lambda t: Trace.from_json(t) if isinstance(t, str) else t)\n    return df\n\n\ndef _extract_request_response_from_trace(df: \"pd.DataFrame\") -> \"pd.DataFrame\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Add `request` and `response`columns from traces if it is not already present.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"trace\" not in df.columns:\n        return df\n\n    def _extract_attribute(trace_data: TraceData, attribute_name: str) -> Any:\n        if att := getattr(trace_data, attribute_name, None):\n            return json.loads(att)\n        return None\n\n    if \"request\" not in df.columns:\n        df[\"request\"] = df[\"trace\"].apply(lambda trace: _extract_attribute(trace.data, \"request\"))\n    if \"response\" not in df.columns:\n        df[\"response\"] = df[\"trace\"].apply(lambda trace: _extract_attribute(trace.data, \"response\"))\n    return df\n\n\ndef _extract_expectations_from_trace(df: \"pd.DataFrame\") -> \"pd.DataFrame\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Add `expectations` columns to the dataframe from assessments\n    stored in the traces, if the \"expectations\" column is not already present.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"trace\" not in df.columns:\n        return df\n\n    expectations_column = []\n    for trace in df[\"trace\"]:\n        expectations = {}\n        for assessment in trace.info.assessments or []:\n            if assessment.expectation is not None:\n                expectations[assessment.name] = assessment.expectation.value\n        expectations_column.append(expectations)\n    # If no trace has assessments, not add the column\n    if all(len(expectations) == 0 for expectations in expectations_column):\n        return df\n\n    df[\"expectations\"] = expectations_column\n    return df\n\n\ndef _convert_scorer_to_legacy_metric(scorer: Scorer) -> EvaluationMetric:\n    \"\"\"\n    Takes in a Scorer object and converts it into a legacy MLflow 2.x\n    Metric object.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        from databricks.agents.evals import metric\n    except ImportError:\n        raise ImportError(\n            \"The `databricks-agents` package is required to use mlflow.genai.evaluate() \"\n            \"Please install it with `pip install databricks-agents`.\"\n        )\n\n    from mlflow.genai.scorers.builtin_scorers import BuiltInScorer\n    from mlflow.types.llm import ChatCompletionRequest\n\n    def eval_fn(\n        request_id: str,\n        request: ChatCompletionRequest | str,\n        response: Any | None,\n        expected_response: Any | None,\n        trace: Trace | None,\n        guidelines: list[str] | dict[str, list[str]] | None,\n        expected_facts: list[str] | None,\n        expected_retrieved_context: list[dict[str, str]] | None,\n        custom_expected: dict[str, Any] | None,\n        **kwargs,\n    ) -> int | float | bool | str | Assessment | list[Assessment]:\n        # Condense all expectations into a single dict\n        expectations = {}\n        if expected_response is not None:\n            expectations[AgentEvaluationReserverKey.EXPECTED_RESPONSE] = expected_response\n        if expected_facts is not None:\n            expectations[AgentEvaluationReserverKey.EXPECTED_FACTS] = expected_facts\n        if expected_retrieved_context is not None:\n            expectations[AgentEvaluationReserverKey.EXPECTED_RETRIEVED_CONTEXT] = (\n                expected_retrieved_context\n            )\n        if guidelines is not None:\n            expectations[AgentEvaluationReserverKey.GUIDELINES] = guidelines\n        if custom_expected is not None:\n            expectations.update(custom_expected)\n\n        merged = {\n            \"inputs\": request,\n            \"outputs\": response,\n            \"expectations\": expectations,\n            \"trace\": trace,\n        }\n        return scorer.run(**merged)\n\n    metric_instance = metric(\n        eval_fn=eval_fn,\n        name=scorer.name,\n    )\n    # Add aggregations as an attribute since the metric decorator doesn't accept it\n    metric_instance.aggregations = scorer.aggregations\n    # Add attribute to indicate if this is a built-in scorer\n    metric_instance._is_builtin_scorer = isinstance(scorer, BuiltInScorer)\n\n    return metric_instance\n\n\ndef standardize_scorer_value(scorer_name: str, value: Any) -> list[Feedback]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Convert the scorer return value to a list of MLflow Assessment (Feedback) objects.\n\n    Scorer can return:\n    - A number, boolean, or string, a list of them.\n    - An Feedback object\n    - A list of Feedback objects\n\n    All of the above will be converted to a list of Feedback objects.\n    \"\"\"\n    # None is a valid metric value, return an empty list\n    if value is None:\n        return []\n\n    # Primitives are valid metric values\n    if isinstance(value, (int, float, bool, str)):\n        return [\n            Feedback(\n                name=scorer_name,\n                source=make_code_type_assessment_source(scorer_name),\n                value=value,\n            )\n        ]\n\n    if isinstance(value, Feedback):\n        value.name = _get_custom_assessment_name(value, scorer_name)\n        return [value]\n\n    if isinstance(value, Collection):\n        assessments = []\n        for item in value:\n            if isinstance(item, Feedback):\n                # Scorer returns multiple assessments as a list.\n                item.name = _get_custom_assessment_name(item, scorer_name)\n                assessments.append(item)\n            else:\n                # If the item is not assessment, the list represents a single assessment\n                # value of list type. Convert it to a Feedback object.\n                assessments.append(\n                    Feedback(\n                        name=scorer_name,\n                        source=make_code_type_assessment_source(scorer_name),\n                        value=item,\n                    )\n                )\n        return assessments\n\n    raise MlflowException.invalid_parameter_value(\n        f\"Got unsupported result from scorer '{scorer_name}'. \"\n        f\"Expected the metric value to be a number, or a boolean, or a string, \"\n        \"or an Feedback, or a list of Feedbacks. \"\n        f\"Got {value}.\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _get_custom_assessment_name(assessment: Feedback, scorer_name: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Get the name of the custom assessment. Use assessment name if present and not a builtin judge\n    name, otherwise use the scorer name.\n\ngrep: tests/evaluation: No such file or directory\n",

Defaults to \"equivalence\".\n        model: {{ model }}\n\n    Example (direct usage):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Equivalence\n\n        # Numerical equivalence\n        assessment = Equivalence()(\n            outputs=42,\n            expectations={\"expected_response\": 42},\n        )\n        print(assessment)  # value: ategoricalRating.YES, rationale: 'Exact numerical match'\n\n        # Text equivalence\n        assessment = Equivalence()(\n            outputs=\"The capital is Paris\",\n            expectations={\"expected_response\": \"Paris is the capital\"},\n        )\n        print(assessment)  # value: CategoricalRating.YES (semantically equivalent)\n\n    Example (with evaluate):\n\n    .. code-block:: python\n\n        import mlflow\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Equivalence\n\n        data = [\n            {\n                \"outputs\": \"The capital is Paris\",\n                \"expectations\": {\"expected_response\": \"Paris\"},\n            }\n        ]\n        result = mlflow.genai.evaluate(data=data, scorers=[Equivalence()])\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str = \"equivalence\"\n    model: str | None = None\n    required_columns: set[str] = {\"outputs\"}\n    description: str = \"Compare outputs against expected outputs for semantic equivalence.\"\n\n    @property\n    def instructions(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Get the instructions of what this scorer evaluates.\"\"\"\n        return EQUIVALENCE_PROMPT_INSTRUCTIONS\n\n    def validate_columns(self, columns: set[str]) -> None:\n        super().validate_columns(columns)\n        if \"expectations/expected_response\" not in columns:\n            raise MissingColumnsException(self.name, {\"expectations/expected_response\"})\n\n    def get_input_fields(self) -> list[JudgeField]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the input fields for the Equivalence scorer.\n\n        Returns:\n            List of JudgeField objects defining the input fields.\n        \"\"\"\n        return [\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"outputs\",\n                description=\"The actual output from the program to compare.\",\n            ),\n            JudgeField(\n                name=\"expectations\",\n                description=(\n                    \"A dictionary containing the expected output. When importing scorers.base, Python first initializes the scorers package (__init__.py)\n# 5. This would try to import builtin_scorers, creating a circular import\n#\n# Solution:\n# - We use Python's __getattr__ mechanism to defer the import of builtin_scorers until\n#   the specific scorer classes are actually accessed at runtime\n# - The TYPE_CHECKING block provides type hints for IDEs and static analysis tools\n#   without actually importing the modules at runtime (TYPE_CHECKING is only True\n#   during static analysis, never during actual execution)\n# - This allows Sphinx autodoc and IDEs to understand the module structure while\n#   avoiding the circular import at runtime\n\n\n# Define the attributes that should be lazily loaded\n_LAZY_IMPORTS = {\n    \"Correctness\",\n    \"ExpectationsGuidelines\",\n    \"Guidelines\",\n    \"Equivalence\",\n    \"RelevanceToQuery\",\n    \"RetrievalGroundedness\",\n    \"RetrievalRelevance\",\n    \"RetrievalSufficiency\",\n    \"Safety\",\n    \"get_all_scorers\",\n}\n\n\ndef __getattr__(name):\n    \"\"\"Lazily import builtin scorers to avoid circular dependency.\"\"\"\n    if name in _LAZY_IMPORTS:\n        # Import the module when first accessed\n        from mlflow.genai.scorers import builtin_scorers\n\n        return getattr(builtin_scorers, name)\n\n    raise AttributeError(f\"module {__name__!r} has no attribute {name!r}\")\n\n\ndef __dir__():\n    \"\"\"Return the list of attributes available in this module.\n\n    This is necessary for Sphinx autodoc and other introspection tools\n    to discover the lazily-loaded scorer classes.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Get the default module attributes\n    module_attrs = list(globals().keys())\n    # Add the lazy imports\n    return sorted(set(module_attrs) | _LAZY_IMPORTS)\n\n\n# The TYPE_CHECKING block below is for static analysis tools only.\n# TYPE_CHECKING is False at runtime, so these imports never execute during normal operation.\n# This gives us the best of both worlds: type hints without circular imports.\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from mlflow.genai.scorers.builtin_scorers import (\n        Correctness,\n        Equivalence,\n        ExpectationsGuidelines,\n        Guidelines,\n        RelevanceToQuery,\n        RetrievalGroundedness,\n        RetrievalRelevance,\n        RetrievalSufficiency,\n        Safety,\n        get_all_scorers,\n    )\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"Correctness\",\n    \"ExpectationsGuidelines\",\n    \"Guidelines\",\n    \"Equivalence\",\n    \"RelevanceToQuery\",\n    \"RetrievalGroundedness\",\n    \"RetrievalRelevance\",\n    \"RetrievalSufficiency\",\n    \"Safety\",\n    \"Scorer\",\n    \"scorer\",\n    \"ScorerSamplingConfig\",\n    \"get_all_scorers\",\n    \"get_scorer\",\n    \"list_scorers\",\n    \"delete_scorer\",\n]\nmlflow/genai/evaluation/telemetry.py:77:    # Check for the legacy custom metrics if databricks-agents is installed\nmlflow/genai/evaluation/utils.py:218:def _convert_scorer_to_legacy_metric(scorer: Scorer) -> EvaluationMetric:\n",

    "output": "\"\"\"Generate the metrics logged into MLflow.\"\"\"\n\nimport collections\nimport logging\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom mlflow.entities.assessment import Feedback\nfrom mlflow.genai.evaluation.entities import EvalResult\nfrom mlflow.genai.judges.builtin import CategoricalRating\nfrom mlflow.genai.scorers.base import AggregationFunc, Scorer\n\n_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n_AGGREGATE_FUNCTIONS = {\n    \"min\": np.min,\n    \"max\": np.max,\n    \"mean\": np.mean,\n    \"median\": np.median,\n    \"variance\": np.var,\n    \"p90\": lambda x: np.percentile(x, 90) if x else None,\n}\n\n\ndef compute_aggregated_metrics(\n    eval_results: list[EvalResult],\n    scorers: list[Scorer],\n) -> dict[str, float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Generates the aggregated per-run metrics from the evaluation result for each row.\n\n    Args:\n        eval_results: List of EvalResult objects\n        scorers: List of scorers\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary of aggregated metrics (metric name -> aggregated value)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Collect assessment values in a flat dictionary\n    assessment_values: dict[str, list[float]] = collections.defaultdict(list)\n    for eval_result in eval_results:\n        for assessment in eval_result.assessments:\n            if isinstance(assessment, Feedback):\n                value = _cast_assessment_value_to_float(assessment)\n                if value is not None:  # Exclude None from aggregation\n                    assessment_values[assessment.name].append(value)\n\n    # List all aggregations to compute for each scorer\n    scorer_aggregations = {}\n    for scorer in scorers:\n        if scorer.aggregations is not None:\n            scorer_aggregations[scorer.name] = scorer.aggregations\n        else:\n            scorer_aggregations[scorer.name] = [\"mean\"]  # default to compute mean only\n\n    # Compute aggregates\n    result = {}\n    for name, values in assessment_values.items():\n        if not values:\n            continue\n\n        # Get the function name from the returned assessment name.\n        scorer_function_name = name.split(\"/\", 1)[-1]\n\n        # Compute aggregations for the scorer, defaulting to just [\"mean\"]\n        aggregations_to_compute = scorer_aggregations.get(scorer_function_name, [\"mean\"])\n        aggregation_results = _compute_aggregations(values, aggregations_to_compute)\n\n        # Each aggregation should be logged as a separate metric\n        for agg_name, agg_value in aggregation_results.items():\n            result[f\"{name}/{agg_name}\"] = agg_value\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef _cast_assessment_value_to_float(assessment: Feedback) -> float | None:\n    \"\"\"Cast the value of an assessment to a float.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(assessment.value, (int, float, bool)):\n        return float(assessment.value)\n    elif (\n        isinstance(assessment.value, str)\n        and CategoricalRating(assessment.value.lower()) != CategoricalRating.UNKNOWN\n    ):\n        return float(assessment.value.lower() == CategoricalRating.YES)\n\n\ndef _compute_aggregations(\n    scores: list[float], aggregations: list[str | AggregationFunc]\n) -> dict[str, float]:\n    \"\"\"Compute aggregate statistics for a list of scores based on specified aggregations.\n\n    Args:\n        scores: List of numeric scores to aggregate\n        aggregations: List of aggregation types to compute (e.g. [\"min\", \"max\", \"mean\"])\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary mapping aggregation names to computed values\n    \"\"\"\n    results = {}\n    for aggregation in aggregations:\n        # Aggregations specified as strings, e.g., \"mean\"\n        if isinstance(aggregation, str):\n            if aggregation not in _AGGREGATE_FUNCTIONS:\n                raise ValueError(f\"Invalid aggregation: {aggregation}\")\n            results[aggregation] = _AGGREGATE_FUNCTIONS[aggregation](scores)\n        # Aggregations specified as a custom function.\n        else:\n            try:\n                results[aggregation.__name__] = aggregation(scores)\n            except Exception as e:\n                _logger.error(f\"Error computing aggregation {aggregation} due to: {e}\")\n                continue\n\n    return results\ndocs/docs/genai/flavors/chat-model-intro/index.mdx:338:You can find the full list of supported parameters <APILink fn=\"mlflow.types.llm.ChatParams\">here</APILink>.\nmlflow/sagemaker/__init__.py:1104:        config: Configuration parameters.
