aggregations=None, description=None, sample_rate=None, filter_string=None)]\npredict_fn = None, model_id = None\n\n    \u001b[0m\u001b[37m@experimental\u001b[39;49;00m(version=\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m3.0.0\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[94mdef\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mevaluate\u001b[39;49;00m(\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        data: \u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33mEvaluationDatasetTypes\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        scorers: \u001b[96mlist\u001b[39;49;00m[Scorer],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        predict_fn: Callable[..., Any] | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        model_id: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    ) -> EvaluationResult:\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m    \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Evaluate the performance of a generative AI model/application using specified\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    data and scorers.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    This function allows you to evaluate a model's performance on a given dataset\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    using various scoring criteria. It supports both built-in scorers provided by\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    MLflow and custom scorers. The evaluation results include metrics and detailed\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    per-row assessments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    There are three different ways to use this function:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **1. Use Traces to evaluate the model/application.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    The `data` parameter takes a DataFrame with `trace` column, which contains a\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    single trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row. This dataframe\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    is easily obtained from the existing traces stored in MLflow, by using the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    :py:func:`mlflow.search_traces` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # model_id is a string starting with \"m-\", e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        trace_df = mlflow.search_traces(model_id=\"<my-model-id>\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=trace_df,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Built-in scorers will understand the model inputs, outputs, and other intermediate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace object. You can also access to\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    the trace object from the custom scorer function by using the `trace` parameter.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        @scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def faster_than_one_second(inputs, outputs, trace):\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return trace.info.execution_duration < 1000\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **2. Use DataFrame or dictionary with \"inputs\", \"outputs\", \"expectations\" columns.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations (ground truth) as\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"I don't know\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"Spark is a data engine\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **3. Pass `predict_fn` and input samples (and optionally expectations).**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    If you want to generate the outputs and traces on-the-fly from your input samples,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    you can pass a callable to the `predict_fn` parameter. In this case, MLflow will\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    pass the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. Therefore, the \"inputs\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    column must be a dictionary with the parameter names as keys.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import openai\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Create a dataframe with input samples\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Define a predict function to evaluate. The \"inputs\" column will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # passed to the prediction function as keyword arguments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def predict_fn(question: str) -> str:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                model=\"gpt-4o-mini\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return response.choices[0].message.content\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            predict_fn=predict_fn,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Args:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data: Dataset for the evaluation. Must be one of the following formats:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * An EvaluationDataset entity\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Pandas DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Spark DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * List of dictionaries\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The dataset must include either of the following columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            1. `trace` column that contains a single trace object corresponding\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                to the prediction for the row.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                If this column is present, MLflow extracts inputs, outputs, assessments,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                and other intermediate information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                object and uses them for scoring. When this column is present, the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                `predict_fn` parameter must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            2. `inputs`, `outputs`, `expectations` columns.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations(ground\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                truth) as a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - inputs (required): Column containing inputs for evaluation. The value\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  must be a dictionary. When `predict_fn` is provided, MLflow will pass\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. For example,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * predict_fn: `def predict_fn(question: str, context: str) -> str`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * inputs: `{\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\", \"context\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\"}`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * `predict_fn` will receive \"What is MLflow?\" as the first argument\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    (`question`) and \"MLflow is an ML platform\" as the second argument (`context`)\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - outputs (optional): Column containing model or app outputs.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  If this column is present, `predict_fn` must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - expectations (optional): Column containing a dictionary of ground truths.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            For list of dictionaries, each dict should follow the above schema.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            Optional columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - tags (optional): Column containing a dictionary of tags. The tags will be logged\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                                   to the respective traces.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        scorers: A list of Scorer objects that produces evaluation scores from\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            inputs, outputs, and other additional contexts. MLflow provides pre-defined\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers, but you can also define custom ones.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        predict_fn: The target function to be evaluated. The specified function will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            executed for each row in the input dataset, and outputs will be used for\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scoring.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The function must emit a single trace per call. If it doesn't, decorate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            the function with @mlflow.trace decorator to ensure a trace to be emitted.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        model_id: Optional model identifier (e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            to associate with the evaluation results. Can be also set globally via the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            :py:func:`mlflow.set_active_model` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Returns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        An :py:class:`mlflow.models.EvaluationResult~` object.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Note:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is only supported on Databricks. The tracking URI must be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        set to Databricks.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. warning::\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is not thread-safe. Please do not use it in multi-threaded\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        environments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    \"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        is_managed_dataset = \u001b[96misinstance\u001b[39;49;00m(data, (EvaluationDataset, EntityEvaluationDataset))\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n>       scorers = validate_scorers(scorers)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n                  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n\u001b[1m\u001b[31mE       NameError: name 'validate_scorers' is not \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mdummy_scorer\u001b[39;49;00m(inputs, outputs):\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n            \u001b[94mreturn\u001b[39;49;00m scorer_return\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n aggregations=None, description=None, sample_rate=None, filter_string=None)]\npredict_fn = None, model_id = None\n\n    \u001b[0m\u001b[37m@experimental\u001b[39;49;00m(version=\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m3.0.0\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[94mdef\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mevaluate\u001b[39;49;00m(\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        data: \u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33mEvaluationDatasetTypes\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        scorers: \u001b[96mlist\u001b[39;49;00m[Scorer],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        predict_fn: Callable[..., Any] | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        model_id: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    ) -> EvaluationResult:\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m    \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Evaluate the performance of a generative AI model/application using specified\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    data and scorers.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    This function allows you to evaluate a model's performance on a given dataset\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    using various scoring criteria. It supports both built-in scorers provided by\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    MLflow and custom scorers. The evaluation results include metrics and detailed\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    per-row assessments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    There are three different ways to use this function:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **1. Use Traces to evaluate the model/application.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    The `data` parameter takes a DataFrame with `trace` column, which contains a\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    single trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row. This dataframe\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    is easily obtained from the existing traces stored in MLflow, by using the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    :py:func:`mlflow.search_traces` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # model_id is a string starting with \"m-\", e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        trace_df = mlflow.search_traces(model_id=\"<my-model-id>\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=trace_df,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Built-in scorers will understand the model inputs, outputs, and other intermediate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace object. You can also access to\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    the trace object from the custom scorer function by using the `trace` parameter.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        @scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def faster_than_one_second(inputs, outputs, trace):\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return trace.info.execution_duration < 1000\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **2. Use DataFrame or dictionary with \"inputs\", \"outputs\", \"expectations\" columns.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations (ground truth) as\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"I don't know\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"Spark is a data engine\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **3. Pass `predict_fn` and input samples (and optionally expectations).**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    If you want to generate the outputs and traces on-the-fly from your input samples,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    you can pass a callable to the `predict_fn` parameter. In this case, MLflow will\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    pass the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. Therefore, the \"inputs\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    column must be a dictionary with the parameter names as keys.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import openai\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Create a dataframe with input samples\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Define a predict function to evaluate. The \"inputs\" column will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # passed to the prediction function as keyword arguments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def predict_fn(question: str) -> str:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                model=\"gpt-4o-mini\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return response.choices[0].message.content\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            predict_fn=predict_fn,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Args:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data: Dataset for the evaluation. Must be one of the following formats:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * An EvaluationDataset entity\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Pandas DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Spark DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * List of dictionaries\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The dataset must include either of the following columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            1. `trace` column that contains a single trace object corresponding\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                to the prediction for the row.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                If this column is present, MLflow extracts inputs, outputs, assessments,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                and other intermediate information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                object and uses them for scoring. When this column is present, the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                `predict_fn` parameter must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            2. `inputs`, `outputs`, `expectations` columns.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations(ground\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                truth) as a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - inputs (required): Column containing inputs for evaluation. The value\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  must be a dictionary. When `predict_fn` is provided, MLflow will pass\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. For example,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * predict_fn: `def predict_fn(question: str, context: str) -> str`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * inputs: `{\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\", \"context\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\"}`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * `predict_fn` will receive \"What is MLflow?\" as the first argument\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    (`question`) and \"MLflow is an ML platform\" as the second argument (`context`)\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - outputs (optional): Column containing model or app outputs.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  If this column is present, `predict_fn` must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - expectations (optional): Column containing a dictionary of ground truths.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            For list of dictionaries, each dict should follow the above schema.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            Optional columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - tags (optional): Column containing a dictionary of tags. The tags will be logged\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                                   to the respective traces.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        scorers: A list of Scorer objects that produces evaluation scores from\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            inputs, outputs, and other additional contexts. MLflow provides pre-defined\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers, but you can also define custom ones.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        predict_fn: The target function to be evaluated. The specified function will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            executed for each row in the input dataset, and outputs will be used for\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scoring.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The function must emit a single trace per call. If it doesn't, decorate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            the function with @mlflow.trace decorator to ensure a trace to be emitted.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        model_id: Optional model identifier (e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            to associate with the evaluation results. Can be also set globally via the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            :py:func:`mlflow.set_active_model` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Returns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        An :py:class:`mlflow.models.EvaluationResult~` object.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Note:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is only supported on Databricks. The tracking URI must be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        set to Databricks.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. warning::\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is not thread-safe. Please do not use it in multi-threaded\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        environments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    \"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        is_managed_dataset = \u001b[96misinstance\u001b[39;49;00m(data, (EvaluationDataset, EntityEvaluationDataset))\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n>       scorers = validate_scorers(scorers)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n                  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n\u001b[1m\u001b[31mE       NameError: name 'validate_scorers' is not sure, just a guess\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m),\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n            ],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        ],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mdummy_scorer\u001b[39;49;00m(inputs, outputs):\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n            \u001b[94mreturn\u001b[39;49;00m scorer_return\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n \u001b[0m\u001b[37m@experimental\u001b[39;49;00m(version=\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m3.0.0\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[94mdef\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mevaluate\u001b[39;49;00m(\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        data: \u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33mEvaluationDatasetTypes\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        scorers: \u001b[96mlist\u001b[39;49;00m[Scorer],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        predict_fn: Callable[..., Any] | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        model_id: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    ) -> EvaluationResult:\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m    \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Evaluate the performance of a generative AI model/application using specified\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    data and scorers.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    This function allows you to evaluate a model's performance on a given dataset\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    using various scoring criteria. It supports both built-in scorers provided by\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    MLflow and custom scorers. The evaluation results include metrics and detailed\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    per-row assessments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    There are three different ways to use this function:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **1. Use Traces to evaluate the model/application.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    The `data` parameter takes a DataFrame with `trace` column, which contains a\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    single trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row. This dataframe\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    is easily obtained from the existing traces stored in MLflow, by using the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    :py:func:`mlflow.search_traces` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # model_id is a string starting with \"m-\", e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        trace_df = mlflow.search_traces(model_id=\"<my-model-id>\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=trace_df,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Built-in scorers will understand the model inputs, outputs, and other intermediate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace object. You can also access to\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    the trace object from the custom scorer function by using the `trace` parameter.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        @scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def faster_than_one_second(inputs, outputs, trace):\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return trace.info.execution_duration < 1000\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **2. Use DataFrame or dictionary with \"inputs\", \"outputs\", \"expectations\" columns.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations (ground truth) as\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"I don't know\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"Spark is a data engine\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **3. Pass `predict_fn` and input samples (and optionally expectations).**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    If you want to generate the outputs and traces on-the-fly from your input samples,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    you can pass a callable to the `predict_fn` parameter. In this case, MLflow will\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    pass the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. Therefore, the \"inputs\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    column must be a dictionary with the parameter names as keys.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import openai\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Create a dataframe with input samples\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Define a predict function to evaluate. The \"inputs\" column will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # passed to the prediction function as keyword arguments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def predict_fn(question: str) -> str:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                model=\"gpt-4o-mini\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return response.choices[0].message.content\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            predict_fn=predict_fn,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Args:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data: Dataset for the evaluation. Must be one of the following formats:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * An EvaluationDataset entity\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Pandas DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Spark DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * List of dictionaries\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The dataset must include either of the following columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            1. `trace` column that contains a single trace object corresponding\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                to the prediction for the row.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                If this column is present, MLflow extracts inputs, outputs, assessments,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                and other intermediate information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                object and uses them for scoring. When this column is present, the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                `predict_fn` parameter must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            2. `inputs`, `outputs`, `expectations` columns.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations(ground\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                truth) as a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - inputs (required): Column containing inputs for evaluation. The value\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  must be a dictionary. When `predict_fn` is provided, MLflow will pass\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. For example,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * predict_fn: `def predict_fn(question: str, context: str) -> str`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * inputs: `{\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\", \"context\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\"}`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * `predict_fn` will receive \"What is MLflow?\" as the first argument\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    (`question`) and \"MLflow is an ML platform\" as the second argument (`context`)\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - outputs (optional): Column containing model or app outputs.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  If this column is present, `predict_fn` must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - expectations (optional): Column containing a dictionary of ground truths.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            For list of dictionaries, each dict should follow the above schema.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            Optional columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - tags (optional): Column containing a dictionary of tags. The tags will be logged\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                                   to the respective traces.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        scorers: A list of Scorer objects that produces evaluation scores from\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            inputs, outputs, and other additional contexts. MLflow provides pre-defined\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers, but you can also define custom ones.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        predict_fn: The target function to be evaluated. The specified function will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            executed for each row in the input dataset, and outputs will be used for\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scoring.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The function must emit a single trace per call. If it doesn't, decorate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            the function with @mlflow.trace decorator to ensure a trace to be emitted.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        model_id: Optional model identifier (e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            to associate with the evaluation results. Can be also set globally via the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            :py:func:`mlflow.set_active_model` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Returns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        An :py:class:`mlflow.models.EvaluationResult~` object.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Note:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is only supported on Databricks. The tracking URI must be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        set to Databricks.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. warning::\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is not thread-safe. Please do not use it in multi-threaded\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        environments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    \"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        is_managed_dataset = \u001b[96misinstance\u001b[39;49;00m(data, (EvaluationDataset, EntityEvaluationDataset))\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n>       scorers = validate_scorers(scorers)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n                  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n\u001b[1m\u001b[31mE       NameError: name 'validate_scorers' is not \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mdummy_scorer\u001b[39;49;00m(inputs, outputs):\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n            \u001b[94mreturn\u001b[39;49;00m scorer_return\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n \u001b[0m\u001b[37m@experimental\u001b[39;49;00m(version=\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m3.0.0\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[94mdef\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mevaluate\u001b[39;49;00m(\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        data: \u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33mEvaluationDatasetTypes\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        scorers: \u001b[96mlist\u001b[39;49;00m[Scorer],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        predict_fn: Callable[..., Any] | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        model_id: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    ) -> EvaluationResult:\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m    \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Evaluate the performance of a generative AI model/application using specified\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    data and scorers.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    This function allows you to evaluate a model's performance on a given dataset\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    using various scoring criteria. It supports both built-in scorers provided by\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    MLflow and custom scorers. The evaluation results include metrics and detailed\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    per-row assessments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    There are three different ways to use this function:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **1. Use Traces to evaluate the model/application.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    The `data` parameter takes a DataFrame with `trace` column, which contains a\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    single trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row. This dataframe\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    is easily obtained from the existing traces stored in MLflow, by using the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    :py:func:`mlflow.search_traces` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # model_id is a string starting with \"m-\", e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        trace_df = mlflow.search_traces(model_id=\"<my-model-id>\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=trace_df,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Built-in scorers will understand the model inputs, outputs, and other intermediate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace object. You can also access to\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    the trace object from the custom scorer function by using the `trace` parameter.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        @scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def faster_than_one_second(inputs, outputs, trace):\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return trace.info.execution_duration < 1000\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **2. Use DataFrame or dictionary with \"inputs\", \"outputs\", \"expectations\" columns.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations (ground truth) as\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"I don't know\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"Spark is a data engine\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **3. Pass `predict_fn` and input samples (and optionally expectations).**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    If you want to generate the outputs and traces on-the-fly from your input samples,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    you can pass a callable to the `predict_fn` parameter. In this case, MLflow will\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    pass the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. Therefore, the \"inputs\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    column must be a dictionary with the parameter names as keys.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import openai\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Create a dataframe with input samples\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Define a predict function to evaluate. The \"inputs\" column will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # passed to the prediction function as keyword arguments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def predict_fn(question: str) -> str:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                model=\"gpt-4o-mini\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return response.choices[0].message.content\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            predict_fn=predict_fn,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Args:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data: Dataset for the evaluation. Must be one of the following formats:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * An EvaluationDataset entity\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Pandas DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Spark DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * List of dictionaries\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The dataset must include either of the following columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            1. `trace` column that contains a single trace object corresponding\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                to the prediction for the row.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                If this column is present, MLflow extracts inputs, outputs, assessments,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                and other intermediate information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                object and uses them for scoring. When this column is present, the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                `predict_fn` parameter must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            2. `inputs`, `outputs`, `expectations` columns.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations(ground\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                truth) as a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - inputs (required): Column containing inputs for evaluation. The value\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  must be a dictionary. When `predict_fn` is provided, MLflow will pass\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. For example,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * predict_fn: `def predict_fn(question: str, context: str) -> str`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * inputs: `{\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\", \"context\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\"}`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * `predict_fn` will receive \"What is MLflow?\" as the first argument\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    (`question`) and \"MLflow is an ML platform\" as the second argument (`context`)\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - outputs (optional): Column containing model or app outputs.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  If this column is present, `predict_fn` must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - expectations (optional): Column containing a dictionary of ground truths.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            For list of dictionaries, each dict should follow the above schema.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            Optional columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - tags (optional): Column containing a dictionary of tags. The tags will be logged\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                                   to the respective traces.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        scorers: A list of Scorer objects that produces evaluation scores from\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            inputs, outputs, and other additional contexts. MLflow provides pre-defined\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers, but you can also define custom ones.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        predict_fn: The target function to be evaluated. The specified function will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            executed for each row in the input dataset, and outputs will be used for\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scoring.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The function must emit a single trace per call. If it doesn't, decorate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            the function with @mlflow.trace decorator to ensure a trace to be emitted.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        model_id: Optional model identifier (e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            to associate with the evaluation results. Can be also set globally via the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            :py:func:`mlflow.set_active_model` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Returns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        An :py:class:`mlflow.models.EvaluationResult~` object.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Note:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is only supported on Databricks. The tracking URI must be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        set to Databricks.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. warning::\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is not thread-safe. Please do not use it in multi-threaded\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        environments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    \"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        is_managed_dataset = \u001b[96misinstance\u001b[39;49;00m(data, (EvaluationDataset, EntityEvaluationDataset))\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n>       scorers = validate_scorers(scorers)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n                  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n\u001b[1m\u001b[31mE       NameError: name 'validate_scorers' is not \u001b[0m\u001b[37m@experimental\u001b[39;49;00m(version=\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m3.0.0\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m)\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[94mdef\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[90m \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[92mevaluate\u001b[39;49;00m(\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        data: \u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33mEvaluationDatasetTypes\u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        scorers: \u001b[96mlist\u001b[39;49;00m[Scorer],\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        predict_fn: Callable[..., Any] | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        model_id: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    ) -> EvaluationResult:\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[90m    \u001b[39;49;00m\u001b[33m\"\"\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Evaluate the performance of a generative AI model/application using specified\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    data and scorers.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    This function allows you to evaluate a model's performance on a given dataset\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    using various scoring criteria. It supports both built-in scorers provided by\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    MLflow and custom scorers. The evaluation results include metrics and detailed\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    per-row assessments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    There are three different ways to use this function:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **1. Use Traces to evaluate the model/application.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    The `data` parameter takes a DataFrame with `trace` column, which contains a\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    single trace object corresponding to the prediction for the row. This dataframe\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    is easily obtained from the existing traces stored in MLflow, by using the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    :py:func:`mlflow.search_traces` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # model_id is a string starting with \"m-\", e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        trace_df = mlflow.search_traces(model_id=\"<my-model-id>\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=trace_df,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Built-in scorers will understand the model inputs, outputs, and other intermediate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace object. You can also access to\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    the trace object from the custom scorer function by using the `trace` parameter.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        @scorer\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def faster_than_one_second(inputs, outputs, trace):\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return trace.info.execution_duration < 1000\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **2. Use DataFrame or dictionary with \"inputs\", \"outputs\", \"expectations\" columns.**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations (ground truth) as\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import pandas as pd\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"outputs\": \"I don't know\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    \"expectations\": \"Spark is a data engine\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                },\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    **3. Pass `predict_fn` and input samples (and optionally expectations).**\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    If you want to generate the outputs and traces on-the-fly from your input samples,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    you can pass a callable to the `predict_fn` parameter. In this case, MLflow will\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    pass the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. Therefore, the \"inputs\"\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    column must be a dictionary with the parameter names as keys.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. code-block:: python\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import mlflow\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        from mlflow.genai.scorers import Correctness, Safety\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        import openai\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Create a dataframe with input samples\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data = pd.DataFrame(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            [\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                {\"inputs\": {\"question\": \"What is Spark?\"}},\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            ]\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # Define a predict function to evaluate. The \"inputs\" column will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        # passed to the prediction function as keyword arguments.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        def predict_fn(question: str) -> str:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            response = openai.OpenAI().chat.completions.create(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                model=\"gpt-4o-mini\",\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": question}],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            return response.choices[0].message.content\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        mlflow.genai.evaluate(\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            data=data,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            predict_fn=predict_fn,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers=[Correctness(), Safety()],\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        )\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Args:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        data: Dataset for the evaluation. Must be one of the following formats:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * An EvaluationDataset entity\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Pandas DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * Spark DataFrame\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            * List of dictionaries\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The dataset must include either of the following columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            1. `trace` column that contains a single trace object corresponding\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                to the prediction for the row.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                If this column is present, MLflow extracts inputs, outputs, assessments,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                and other intermediate information e.g. retrieved context, from the trace\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                object and uses them for scoring. When this column is present, the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                `predict_fn` parameter must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            2. `inputs`, `outputs`, `expectations` columns.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                Alternatively, you can pass inputs, outputs, and expectations(ground\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                truth) as a column in the dataframe (or equivalent list of dictionaries).\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - inputs (required): Column containing inputs for evaluation. The value\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  must be a dictionary. When `predict_fn` is provided, MLflow will pass\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  the inputs to the `predict_fn` as keyword arguments. For example,\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * predict_fn: `def predict_fn(question: str, context: str) -> str`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * inputs: `{\"question\": \"What is MLflow?\", \"context\": \"MLflow is an ML platform\"}`\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  * `predict_fn` will receive \"What is MLflow?\" as the first argument\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                    (`question`) and \"MLflow is an ML platform\" as the second argument (`context`)\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - outputs (optional): Column containing model or app outputs.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                  If this column is present, `predict_fn` must not be provided.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - expectations (optional): Column containing a dictionary of ground truths.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            For list of dictionaries, each dict should follow the above schema.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            Optional columns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                - tags (optional): Column containing a dictionary of tags. The tags will be logged\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m                                   to the respective traces.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        scorers: A list of Scorer objects that produces evaluation scores from\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            inputs, outputs, and other additional contexts. MLflow provides pre-defined\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scorers, but you can also define custom ones.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        predict_fn: The target function to be evaluated. The specified function will be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            executed for each row in the input dataset, and outputs will be used for\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            scoring.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            The function must emit a single trace per call. If it doesn't, decorate\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            the function with @mlflow.trace decorator to ensure a trace to be emitted.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        model_id: Optional model identifier (e.g. \"m-074689226d3b40bfbbdf4c3ff35832cd\")\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            to associate with the evaluation results. Can be also set globally via the\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m            :py:func:`mlflow.set_active_model` function.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Returns:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        An :py:class:`mlflow.models.EvaluationResult~` object.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    Note:\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is only supported on Databricks. The tracking URI must be\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        set to Databricks.\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m    .. warning::\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m\u001b[39;49;00m\n    \u001b[33m        This function is not thread-safe.      *,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        name: \u001b[96mstr\u001b[39;49;00m | \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m = \u001b[94mNone\u001b[39;49;00m,\u001b[90m\u001b[39;49;00m\n        description:
